    image: redpandadata/redpanda:latest

              --kafka-addr, "PLAINTEXT://0.0.0.0:9092", --advertise-kafka-addr, "PLAINTEXT://redpanda:9092"]
    ports: ["9092:9092"]
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD-SHELL", "rpk cluster health | grep -q 'Healthy:.*true'"]
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 10

  redis:
    image: redis:7
    ports: ["6379:6379"]
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "redis-cli", "ping"]
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 3s
      retries: 10

    command: mlflow server --host 0.0.0.0 --port 5000 --backend-store-uri sqlite:///mlflow/mlflow.db --default-artifact-root /mlflow/artifacts
    volumes: ["./mlruns:/mlflow"]
    ports: ["5000:5000"]
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "python", "-c", "import urllib.request; urllib.request.urlopen('http://localhost:5000/health')"]
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 10

  api:
    build: .

      KAFKA_BROKER: redpanda:9092

    ports: ["8000:8000"]

      redis: {condition: service_healthy}
      mlflow: {condition: service_healthy}

  recorder:
    build: .
    entrypoint: ["python", "-m", "sentinalops.data.recorder"]

      RECORDER_SYMBOLS: BTCUSDT
      RECORDER_DATA_DIR: /app/data/raw/binance

    ports: ["9108:9108"]
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "python", "-c", "import urllib.request; urllib.request.urlopen('http://localhost:9108/metrics')"]
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 3
    restart: unless-stopped
    stop_grace_period: 30s   # time to flush buffered rows on SIGTERM

  prometheus:
    image: prom/prometheus:latest
    volumes: ["./infra/prometheus.yml:/etc/prometheus/prometheus.yml:ro"]
    ports: ["9090:9090"]

    ports: ["3000:3000"]
    depends_on: [prometheus]


=== Dockerfile ===
FROM python:3.11-slim
ENV PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 PIP_NO_CACHE_DIR=1
WORKDIR /app
COPY pyproject.toml ./
COPY src ./src
RUN pip install -e .
COPY configs ./configs
COPY scripts ./scripts
RUN chmod +x scripts/entrypoint.sh
EXPOSE 8000
HEALTHCHECK --interval=10s --timeout=3s --start-period=20s --retries=5 \
  CMD python -c "import urllib.request,sys; sys.exit(0 if urllib.request.urlopen('http://localhost:8000/health').status==200 else 1)"
ENTRYPOINT ["scripts/entrypoint.sh"]
CMD ["uvicorn", "sentinalops.serving.app:create_app", "--factory", "--host", "0.0.0.0", "--port", "8000"]


=== Makefile ===
.PHONY: install test train api up down record audit features
install:
	pip install -e ".[dev]"
test:
	pytest -q
train:
	python -m sentinalops.training.train_baseline
api:
	uvicorn sentinalops.serving.app:create_app --factory --reload
record:
	python -m sentinalops.data.recorder
audit:
	python -m sentinalops.data.audit_recording --hours 24
features:
	python -m sentinalops.data.pipeline
up:
	docker compose up -d
down:
	docker compose down -v


=== MODEL_BUILD_PROMPT.md ===
# Task: build a plug-and-play, high-performing liquidity-stress forecaster for SentinalOps

You are working in the SentinalOps repository (https://github.com/kushalradia2007/SentinalOps, Python 3.11+, package in `src/sentinalops/`). Read `README.md`, `docs/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/labels.md`, `docs/pipeline.md`, `docs/healing.md` and `docs/streaming.md` before writing code. Everything below is grounded in that code; if anything here disagrees with the code, trust the code and say so.

## 1. What the system does (do not change it)

SentinalOps is a streaming ML system that watches Binance's public BTCUSDT order book (`depth5@100ms`) and trades (`aggTrade`). Once a second it predicts the probability that **liquidity stress starts within the next 30 seconds**. It is not a trading bot and does not predict prices.

Flow: recorder -> offline pipeline / stream processor (34 features per second) -> model -> `/score` API and stream processor -> prediction ledger -> outcome verifier (labels each prediction 30 s later) -> heal loop (detects degraded live performance, retrains a challenger, runs promotion gates, promotes with a shadow period, rolls back if worse).

**The owner's core requirement:** the whole system stays untouched. Only the model is swappable. Upgrading the model in future must mean "add a new model, change one config line" â€” no edits to pipeline, serving, streaming, ledger, verifier or heal code. And the heal loop must retrain and improve **whatever model is currently installed** (same model type, same recipe, fresh data), never silently replace it with a different model type.

## 2. The prediction task, exactly

- **Input:** one feature tick = 34 floats, in this order (`sentinalops.features.microstructure.FEATURE_ORDER`, also `DomainConfig.feature_names`):
  - instant: `rel_spread, bid_depth, ask_depth, depth_top5, depth_imbalance, l1_imbalance`
  - for each window w in {1, 5, 30, 60} s: `trade_intensity_{w}s, trade_volume_{w}s, trade_imbalance_{w}s, realized_vol_{w}s, mid_return_{w}s, depth_change_{w}s, spread_change_{w}s`
- **Label** (`src/sentinalops/labels.py`, `docs/labels.md`): 1 if confirmed stress starts in `(t, t+30 s]`, else 0. Stress = top-5 depth below `depth_fraction` x its 600 s trailing median (primary) or relative spread above `spread_multiple` x its trailing median (secondary), confirmed over 2 consecutive ticks. Only ticks that are calm at `t` are eligible (onset forecasting). Rows with `label_status != "ok"` (`not_calm`, `void_unknown`, `void_gap`) must never be used for training or scoring.
- **Training table** (`sentinalops.data.pipeline.read_table(out_dir)`): columns `symbol, ts_ms, <34 features>, label, label_status, time_to_onset_s, fwd_max_rel_spread, fwd_min_depth_ratio, regime, source`. One row per second.
- **Positives are rare** (expected roughly 0.2%â€“20%). Accuracy is meaningless here. "Highly accurate" means: high **average precision (PR-AUC)**, high recall at a low false-positive rate, good calibration, useful lead time â€” and clearly beating the persistence baseline.

## 3. How models are wired today (what you must generalise)

The model contract is implicit and scattered. Today a model is any joblib-pickled object with `predict_proba(DataFrame) -> (n, 2)`, `feature_names_in_` equal to `FEATURE_ORDER`, and a writable attribute `sentinal_version_` (stamped by the registry). Touchpoints:

- `src/sentinalops/serving/model.py` â€” `ChampionModel`: loads `champion.joblib`, hot-reloads on file change, refuses a model whose `feature_names_in_` differs, calls `predict_proba(row)[:, 1]`.
- `src/sentinalops/serving/app.py` â€” `/score` uses `ChampionModel`.
- `src/sentinalops/stream/processor.py` â€” scores with champion and shadow `ChampionModel`s.
- `src/sentinalops/heal/registry.py` â€” `joblib.dump/load`, sets `model.sentinal_version_`, atomic champion/shadow swaps.
- `src/sentinalops/heal/retrain.py` â€” **hard-codes `from sentinalops.training.train_baseline import fit`** for challengers; gates call `predict_proba(df[names])[:, 1]`.
- `src/sentinalops/simulator/heal_sim.py`, `scripts/entrypoint.sh` (checks `feature_names_in_`, trains a baseline if no model), and tests.
- `src/sentinalops/training/train_baseline.py` â€” current model: `HistGradientBoostingClassifier(max_iter=200, learning_rate=0.08)`, trained on synthetic data.

## 4. Part A â€” the one-time "model slot" refactor (the only system change allowed)

Make the smallest change that turns the model into a plug-in, then never touch system code again for model upgrades.

1. **A model package per model**, e.g. `src/sentinalops/models/<model_name>/`, plus `src/sentinalops/models/base.py` defining the contract and `src/sentinalops/models/__init__.py` with a registry `get_recipe(name)`.
2. **The contract** (keep it compatible with the existing callers so serving, processor, registry and gates need no changes):
   - A *recipe* with `fit(train_df: DataFrame, cfg: DomainConfig, seed: int) -> fitted model`. The recipe owns everything about training: preprocessing, in-model derived features, class weighting, hyperparameters, internal validation, early stopping, calibration.
   - The *fitted model* is a picklable object exposing `predict_proba(X: DataFrame) -> ndarray (n, 2)`, `classes_ = [0, 1]`, `feature_names_in_` equal to `cfg.feature_names` (it receives exactly the 34 raw features and nothing else), and allows setting `sentinal_version_`.
   - Metadata on the fitted model, as attributes that survive pickling: `sentinal_model_name_` (the recipe name), `sentinal_model_params_`, recipe version, training data window, library versions. Also store `model_name` and `params` in the registry's version metadata when a model is registered.
   - **Robust on small or odd data:** `fit` must never crash the heal loop because of data shape. If `ts_ms` is absent (e.g. `make_ticks` output used by `tests/test_thesis.py` and `tests/test_train.py`), the data is small, or the post-embargo calibration slice lacks either class (or has fewer than ~20 positives), fall back to an uncalibrated fit (or Platt scaling on an inner chronological split) and record which path was taken in the metadata. Only "no positives or no negatives at all" may raise, as `train_challenger` already does.
3. **Selection by config:** add a `model:` block to `configs/domain_liquidity.yaml` and the `DomainConfig` schema (`src/sentinalops/config.py`; the schema rejects unknown keys), e.g. `model: {name: gbm_v1, params: {}}`. Default must reproduce today's HistGradientBoosting behaviour so every existing test still passes. `model.name` is used for **manual** training and upgrades only (the training command in step 5).
4. **Heal loop improves the installed model, whatever the config says:** change `heal/retrain.py::train_challenger` so it retrains with the recipe of the **current champion**: read `sentinal_model_name_` and `sentinal_model_params_` from the champion object (the controller already loads it via `registry.load(champion)` in `heal/controller.py::_retrain`; pass it in), and fall back to `cfg.model.name` only when the champion carries no such metadata (models trained before this refactor). This way, editing the YAML before a manual promotion can never make a heal cycle silently switch model types. Keep this change minimal; nothing else in the heal loop changes. Add a test: champion is recipe A, YAML says recipe B, heal retrains recipe A.
5. **One generic training entry point:** `python -m sentinalops.training.train --model <name> [--data synthetic|<table dir>] --out models/champion.joblib`, used by `scripts/entrypoint.sh` instead of `train_baseline` (keep `train_baseline` working as a thin wrapper so tests and imports don't break).
6. **Pickle safety:** pickles reference Python class paths. A released model package is frozen: an upgrade is a new package (`gbm_v2/`), never an edit of `gbm_v1/`, so older registry versions and rollbacks keep loading. Document this rule.
7. **Upgrade procedure** must be exactly: add `src/sentinalops/models/<new_name>/`, set `model.name` in the YAML, run the training command, register/promote. Write it in `docs/models.md` as a 3-step guide.

## 5. Part B â€” the high-performing model

Build and compare candidates as separate model packages behind the contract above. CPU only, no GPU.

- **Baselines (must exist, every report compares against them):**
  - constant base rate;
  - persistence baseline (chunk 26.4): "the thinner the book is now relative to its recent reference, the likelier stress is". It exists in two forms, kept separate:
    - a **registered model** behind the contract, which sees only the 34 features, so it is a fixed monotone score built from them (e.g. from `depth_change_60s`, `depth_change_30s`, `spread_change_60s`, `rel_spread`), with no fitting beyond optional calibration;
    - an **offline comparator** inside `evaluate.py` only, which may read extra columns or recompute depth vs its 600 s trailing reference with `labels.py` (see `base_rate_report`'s lazy baseline). It is never registered or served.
  - logistic regression on standardised features.
- **Strong candidates:** tuned HistGradientBoosting; LightGBM (and optionally XGBoost/CatBoost) if the gain justifies a dependency; a small calibrated ensemble (e.g. averaged GBM + logistic) only if it clearly wins.
- **Inside the recipe you may:** add derived features computed only from the 34 inputs of the same tick (ratios, logs, signed products, short-vs-long window contrasts such as `trade_intensity_1s / trade_intensity_60s`), handle class imbalance (weights, not resampling across time), tune hyperparameters, early-stop, and calibrate probabilities (isotonic or Platt) on a **later** validation period after an embargo.
- **Not allowed:** changing the 34 features, the label, the pipeline or the stream processor; stateful or sequence models that need previous ticks (the API scores one tick at a time; sequence models are out of scope for v1); anything that reads data after `t`.
- **Hard limits:** single-tick CPU inference p99 under 10 ms; model file under 50 MB; fit on 1 month of 1 Hz data (~2.6M rows) in under 15 minutes on 24 CPU cores and 15 GB RAM.
- **Latency discipline:** serving calls `predict_proba` with a 1-row DataFrame (`ChampionModel.predict`, which you must not change). Inside `predict_proba`, convert to a contiguous NumPy array once (`X[feature_names].to_numpy(dtype=np.float32)` or float64 if a model needs it), compute derived features with NumPy, and run inner estimators on arrays, not pandas columns. Measure latency by timing many **single-row calls through `ChampionModel.predict`**, never batch time divided by n.
- **Memory discipline:** filter to `label_status == "ok"` before building derived features, cast feature matrices to float32 for training, and avoid copying the full frame for calibration or CV splits (use index slices). Check that float32 keeps enough precision for the tiny-valued features (`rel_spread` ~1e-7, `mid_return_1s`), e.g. by comparing float32 vs float64 predictions on a sample.

## 6. Evaluation rules (non-negotiable â€” leakage makes numbers worthless)

- Rows are 1 s apart and labels look 30 s ahead, so neighbouring rows share outcomes. **Never use random or shuffled splits.** Use chronological splits and **purged walk-forward cross-validation** with an embargo of at least `split_embargo_seconds` (60 s) between train and test folds.
- Label thresholds (`depth_fraction`, `spread_multiple`) must be fitted on the training period only (`labels.fit_thresholds`), never on test data.
- Hyperparameters are chosen on validation folds only; the final test period is touched once.
- Metrics module (`src/sentinalops/training/metrics.py` or similar, unit-tested on toy examples): average precision (primary, matches `promotion_metric: avg_precision`), ROC-AUC, recall at 1% FPR, precision/recall at the configured `alert_threshold`, Brier score, reliability table (10 bins), lift over base rate, false alarms per hour, detection lead time (seconds between first alert and stress onset, using `time_to_onset_s`), per-regime breakdown (`regime` column), and single-tick inference latency p50/p95/p99.
- An evaluation runner, e.g. `python -m sentinalops.training.evaluate --data <synthetic|table dir> --models all`, prints one comparison table and writes `docs/results.md` plus a JSON report.
- Also run the existing heal-loop scenario (`tests/test_heal.py`, `simulator/heal_sim.py`) with each candidate as the installed model: the loop must still detect degradation, retrain the *same* model type, pass gates and promote.

## 7. Data available now, and the honesty rule

- **Real data is still being recorded** (started 2026-11-27 on a server; it will take weeks). The repo currently has only synthetic data:
  - `sentinalops.simulator.regimes.make_ticks(regime, n, seed)` â€” i.i.d. feature rows for "calm" and "volatile" regimes, with no real time structure;
  - `sentinalops.simulator.heal_sim.market(segments, start_ms)` â€” those rows laid on a 1 Hz timeline;
  - `tests/pipeline_helpers.make_events(...)` + `write_recording(...)` â€” synthetic raw recordings in the recorder's on-disk layout, which can be run through `python -m sentinalops.data.pipeline` to get a realistic training table end to end.
- **Synthetic numbers are plumbing checks, not accuracy.** The synthetic generator was written by hand; a model that scores well on it has learned the generator. Do **not** tune hyperparameters to synthetic data and do **not** claim any model is accurate from synthetic results. Label every synthetic result as such.
- Build everything so that when real data arrives the owner runs one documented sequence: pipeline on the recording -> fit label thresholds on the training period -> evaluate all candidates with walk-forward CV -> pick the champion by average precision (ties broken by Brier, then latency) -> train it on all training data -> save `models/champion.joblib` -> bootstrap it in the registry. Put that sequence in `docs/models.md`.

## 8. Engineering rules

- Work on a new branch; small, focused commits; one pull request per part (A, then B).
- All existing tests must still pass (`pytest`), plus `ruff check src tests`. Add tests for: the contract (every registered recipe produces a model that `ChampionModel` loads and scores), config selection, heal loop retraining the installed model type, metrics on toy data, purged split with embargo (no train row within the embargo of a test row), and a mutation check showing the leakage test fails if the embargo is removed.
- Add new dependencies only when needed, to `pyproject.toml` (core dependencies if the served champion needs them, so the Docker image has them), with a one-line justification.
- Write `docs/models.md` (contract, how to add/upgrade a model, real-data runbook) and `docs/model-card.md` (template: data period, metrics vs baselines, per-regime results, known limits).

## 9. What to hand back

1. A summary of the Part A diff: every system file you changed and why (it should be a short list).
2. The model packages you added, with their recipes in one paragraph each.
3. The comparison table on synthetic data, clearly marked "synthetic â€” plumbing check only".
4. The exact commands to run once real data exists.
5. Anything in this brief that turned out to be wrong or impossible, and what you did instead.


=== PR_DESCRIPTION.md ===
## Summary

**Model slot.** Models are now plug-in packages under `src/sentinalops/models/` (`gbm_v1` is the original champion). Upgrading a model means adding a package, changing `model.name` in the config and running `python -m sentinalops.training.train`. The heal loop retrains the **champion's own recipe**, so editing the config can never make a heal cycle switch model types. See `docs/models.md`.

**Live wiring.** `python -m sentinalops.live` runs the full loop:

- **Feed:** Binance, reusing the recorder's reconnect, watchdog, rotation and de-duplication through an optional `on_row` hook.
- **Bus â†’ workers:** the existing `StreamProcessor` and `OutcomeVerifier`.
- **Ledger:** append-only, now with WAL, batched writes and deterministic prediction IDs.

The heal service runs as a separate process (`--role heal`) and trains from the ledger. The bus is in-memory by default; `--bus kafka` switches to Redpanda. See `docs/live.md`.

**Oracle Cloud deploy kit** (`deploy/oracle/`):

- a Binance pre-flight check, which runs before anything on the system changes;
- systemd units for the live pipeline and heal services;
- optional Redpanda, with 3 GB reserved to avoid idle reclamation;
- a live monitor.

**Also:**

- `.gitignore` no longer ignores `src/sentinalops/models/`;
- `.gitattributes` keeps Linux line endings for shell, systemd and YAML files.

## No changes to

Feature, label, pipeline and verifier logic. The recorder's behaviour and output are unchanged by default.

## Testing

- 225 tests pass (55 new) and `ruff` is clean.
- A 30-minute synthetic replay through the bus reproduces the offline features (to 1e-9) and labels on every tick.
- 10-minute live run against Binance:
  - 0 errors, 0 late events, 0 reconnects;
  - the heal service registered the champion and the live process hot-reloaded it.
- `KafkaBus` contract tests run only with `KAFKA_BROKER` set; not yet run against a real broker.

## Note

With the placeholder label thresholds, stress fired on about 28% of eligible ticks in the live run. The thresholds need fitting on recorded data (task 9.3) before any model evaluation.

Design and plan: `docs/design/`.

=== README.md ===
# SentinalOps: Liquidity Intelligence

### A self-healing streaming ML platform that monitors market liquidity in real time

[![License](https://img.shields.io/badge/License-MIT-yellow.svg)](https://img.shields.io/badge/License-MIT-yellow.svg)
[![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/python-3.11-blue.svg)](https://img.shields.io/badge/python-3.11-blue.svg)
[![Docker Compose](https://img.shields.io/badge/docker-compose-blue.svg)](https://img.shields.io/badge/docker-compose-blue.svg)

---

## Overview

SentinalOps watches the BTCUSDT order book and trade flow on Binance's public market-data streams and does four things:

1. **Detects** abnormal liquidity conditions as they happen.
2. **Forecasts** the probability that liquidity stress starts within the next 30 seconds.
3. **Verifies** every forecast against what the market did after it was made.
4. **Heals** itself: when the market regime changes and the forecasts get worse, it retrains, checks the new model against the old one, and promotes it only if it is better.

> **One-line pitch:** a streaming ML platform that detects abnormal market conditions, forecasts liquidity stress, grades its own predictions against the future, and adapts when market behaviour changes.

### What this is not

- It is not a trading bot. It places no orders and needs no API keys.
- It does not predict prices.
- It reports measurable market conditions. It makes no claim about manipulation or about why a liquidity event happened.

> **Status:** data layer, stream processor, prediction ledger, outcome verifier and self-healing loop are built. The model is a plug-in (`src/sentinalops/models/`, see [`docs/models.md`](docs/models.md)), and the heal loop retrains the installed model's own recipe. The live system runs end to end from Binance to the ledger and the heal service (`python -m sentinalops.live`, see [`docs/live.md`](docs/live.md)), over an in-memory bus or Redpanda, with an Oracle Cloud deploy kit in `deploy/oracle/`. Next: champion candidates and evaluation on recorded data (chunks 26, 30, 31); see [`docs/ROADMAP.md`](docs/ROADMAP.md).

---

## Why this domain

| Need | How market liquidity provides it |
| --- | --- |
| A real live stream | Public WebSocket feeds of trades and order-book depth, with updates every 100 ms |
| Ground-truth labels | The future stream itself: 30 seconds after a forecast, we can measure whether stress occurred |
| A reason to self-heal | Markets move between regimes (calm, volatile, thin), so a model trained in one degrades in another |

---

## Architecture

```text
â”Œâ”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”
â”‚ Binance public WS    â”‚  aggTrade + depth5@100ms (or replay of recorded data)
â””â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”¬â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”˜
           â–¼
â”Œâ”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”
â”‚ Producers + recorder â”‚  feed health, reconnect, Parquet archive
â””â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”¬â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”˜
           â–¼
â”Œâ”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”
â”‚ Redpanda             â”‚  raw_trades, raw_depth, features, predictions, outcomes
â””â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”¬â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”˜
           â–¼
â”Œâ”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”
â”‚ Stream processor     â”‚  one feature tick per second, rolling 1/5/30/60 s windows
â””â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”¬â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”˜
           â–¼

â””â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”˜
```

---

## What the model predicts

**Liquidity stress** at time `t` means either of:

- **Depth depletion (primary):** top-five order-book depth falls below a fixed fraction of its trailing median.
- **Spread expansion (secondary):** relative spread rises above a fixed multiple of its trailing median.

The thresholds are chosen from training-period data and then frozen. The forecast target is the **onset** of stress within the next 30 seconds, made once per second.

Rules that keep the evaluation honest:

- Features at time `t` use only data up to `t`; the verifier uses only data after `t`.
- Train and test periods are chronological, with an embargo gap of at least the horizon.
- Every model is compared with a persistence baseline ("stressed now, so stressed in 30 seconds").
- Feature drift alone never triggers retraining. A measured drop in matured-label performance does.

### Features (about 10â€“15, each over 1, 5, 30 and 60 second windows)

| Feature | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Mid-price | `(best_bid + best_ask) / 2` |
| Relative spread | `(best_ask - best_bid) / mid_price` |
| Bid / ask depth | Quantity across the top five levels on each side |
| Depth imbalance | `(bid_depth - ask_depth) / (bid_depth + ask_depth)` |
| Trade imbalance | Buy-initiated versus sell-initiated volume |
| Trade intensity | Trades per window |
| Realized volatility | Volatility of recent mid-price returns |
| Depth / spread change | Change over each rolling window |

---

## Approaches compared

| Approach | Methods | Library |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Rolling statistical thresholds | z-scores on spread, depth, imbalance | pandas |
| Batch anomaly detection | Isolation Forest, ECOD, COPOD, autoencoder | scikit-learn, PyOD, PyTorch |
| Online anomaly detection | Half-Space Trees, ADWIN drift | River |
| Supervised stress forecaster | Gradient-boosted trees on verified outcomes | scikit-learn |

## Metrics

| Metric | Why it matters |
| --- | --- |
| PR-AUC, precision, recall | Stress events are rare, so accuracy is meaningless |
| Recall at 1% false-positive rate | Alert quality at a tolerable false-alarm level |
| Brier score and calibration | A forecast of 80% should come true about 80% of the time |
| Detection lead time | Seconds of warning before stress begins |
| False alarms per hour | Drives alert fatigue |
| Per-regime breakdown | Shows where a model fails |
| Inference latency (p50/p95/p99) | The forecast has to arrive before the event |
| Heal time | Reliability alert to promoted model |

---

## Tech stack

| Layer | Choice |
| --- | --- |
| Data source | Binance public Spot market data (WebSocket, no API key) |
| Streaming | Redpanda (Kafka-compatible) |
| Stream processing | Faust or a plain Python consumer |
| Feature store | Feast, with Redis online and Parquet offline |
| Models | scikit-learn, PyOD, River, PyTorch |
| Tracking and registry | MLflow |
| Serving | FastAPI |
| Monitoring | Evidently, Prometheus, Grafana |
| Orchestration | Prefect |
| CI/CD | GitHub Actions |
| Infrastructure | Docker Compose |

---

## Quick start

Prerequisites: Docker with Compose v2, Python 3.11+, Git.

```bash
git clone https://github.com/kushalradia2007/SentinalOps.git
cd SentinalOps
pip install -e ".[dev]"
make test
cp .env.example .env
docker compose up -d --build
```

This brings up the API on `:8000`, MLflow on `:5000`, Prometheus on `:9090` and Grafana on `:3000`. See [`docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`](docs/DEVELOPMENT.md) for what runs today and what is still planned.

---

## The demo

The end-to-end demo replays a recorded period where the market changes regime:

1. **Calm market.** Forecasts mature and the champion beats the persistence baseline.
2. **Regime shift.** Reliability falls and the `ReliabilityDropped` alert fires.
3. **Heal.** The flow retrains a challenger and runs the promotion gates.
4. **Promotion.** The champion alias moves, a "Model Healed" marker appears, and a shadow check guards against a bad promotion.
5. **Recovery.** Reliability returns above the baseline.

A second, shorter demo stops the feed on purpose to show predictions being suspended and resumed.

---

## Scope for v1.0

| In | Out |
| --- | --- |
| One symbol (BTCUSDT), one exchange | Multiple symbols or exchanges |
| Top-five depth snapshots | Full order-book reconstruction |
| 30-second binary stress forecast | Price prediction |
| Monitoring and alerting | Any order placement or trading logic |

  push:
    branches: [main]
  pull_request:

jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    strategy:
      matrix:
        python-version: ["3.11", "3.12"]
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: ${{ matrix.python-version }}
          cache: pip
      - name: Install
        run: pip install -e ".[dev]"
      - name: Lint
        run: ruff check .
      - name: Test
        run: pytest -q

  docker:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    needs: test
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - name: Create .env from example
        run: cp .env.example .env
      - name: Validate compose file
        run: docker compose config -q
      - name: Build API image
        run: docker build -t sentinalops-api .

=== configs\domain_liquidity.yaml ===
# Domain config for SentinalOps: Liquidity Intelligence.
# Every key is typed and range-checked by sentinalops.config.DomainConfig; unknown keys are errors.
domain: market_liquidity
thesis: >
  Market microstructure is non-stationary: detect abnormal liquidity conditions
  in real time, forecast liquidity stress 30 seconds ahead, verify every forecast
  against the future stream, and heal the model when the market regime changes.
sources:
  offline: [recorded_binance_stream, binance_public_archive_trades]
  live: [binance_ws_aggTrade, binance_ws_depth5_100ms]
  delayed_labels: [outcome_verifier]
features_module: sentinalops.features.microstructure
drift_watch: [rel_spread, depth_top5, depth_imbalance, trade_intensity_5s, realized_vol_30s]
drift_threshold: 0.3        # Evidently drift share; drift alone never triggers retraining
retrain_window_hours: 6
promotion_metric: avg_precision
alert_threshold: 0.5        # alert when the stress probability is at least this
model:                      # a package in src/sentinalops/models (see docs/models.md)
  name: gbm_v1              # used for manual training; the heal loop follows the champion's own recipe
  params: {}

symbol: BTCUSDT
prediction_cadence_seconds: 1
label_horizon_seconds: 30   # labels mature this long after the prediction
feature_windows_seconds: [1, 5, 30, 60]
label:
  primary: depth_depletion          # top-5 depth falls below a fraction of its trailing median
  depth_fraction: 0.5               # placeholder; frozen from training data in task 9.3
  secondary: spread_expansion       # spread exceeds a multiple of its trailing median (null = off)
  spread_multiple: 3.0              # placeholder; frozen from training data in task 9.3
  reference_window_seconds: 600
  min_reference_seconds: 300        # reference is not trusted before this much history
  confirm_ticks: 2                  # consecutive stressed ticks before stress counts
  onset_only: true                  # only label transitions from calm into stress
split_embargo_seconds: 60           # gap between train and test, at least the horizon
stale_data_seconds: 3               # suspend predictions when the feed is older than this
heal:                               # the self-healing loop (chunks 39, 42-45)
  metrics_window_minutes: 60        # rolling window for live scores
  min_positives: 20                 # fewer stress events than this in the window: no verdict
  degradation_margin: 0.15          # degraded when live AP < champion's holdout AP - this
  min_matured_labels: 2000          # matured labels since promotion before a retrain
  cooldown_minutes: 60              # no second retrain attempt within this
  replay_fraction: 0.3              # older rows mixed in, relative to the recent rows
  holdout_fraction: 0.2             # newest slice of the window, held out for the gates
  min_gain: 0.05                    # challenger must beat the champion's AP by this
  max_regression: 0.10              # ...and lose at most this much AP on older regimes
  min_lift_over_baseline: 1.5       # ...and reach this multiple of the base rate
  max_brier_increase: 0.01          # ...and not be worse calibrated than this
  shadow_minutes: 30                # old champion keeps scoring in shadow this long
  rollback_margin: 0.05             # roll back if the new champion is worse by this


=== deploy\oracle\monitor.py ===
"""One screen for the live system on the Oracle VM: services, live pipeline, heal loop, ledger, host.

    deploy/oracle/monitor.sh          one snapshot
    deploy/oracle/monitor.sh live     refresh every 10 s (Ctrl-C to quit)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import shutil
import sqlite3
import subprocess
import time
from datetime import UTC, datetime
from pathlib import Path

STATE = Path(os.environ.get("SENTINALOPS_STATE", "/var/lib/sentinalops"))
UNITS = ("sentinalops-live", "sentinalops-heal")


def journal(unit: str) -> str:
    try:
        r = subprocess.run(["journalctl", "-u", unit, "-n", "400", "-o", "cat", "--no-pager"],
                           capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10)
        return r.stdout
    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
        return ""


def last_json(text: str, msg: str) -> dict | None:
    for line in reversed(text.splitlines()):
        if f'"msg": "{msg}"' in line:
            try:
                return json.loads(line)
            except ValueError:
                continue
    return None


def unit_state(unit: str) -> str:
    try:
        r = subprocess.run(["systemctl", "is-active", unit], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5)
        return r.stdout.strip() or "unknown"
    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
        return "unknown"


def age(ts: str | None) -> str:
    if not ts:
        return "never"
    s = time.time() - datetime.fromisoformat(ts.replace("Z", "+00:00")).timestamp()
    return f"{s:.0f}s ago" if s < 120 else f"{s / 60:.0f}m ago"


def ledger_section(data_dir: Path) -> list[str]:
    path = data_dir / "ledger.sqlite"
    if not path.exists():
        return ["no ledger yet"]
    now = int(time.time() * 1000)
    hour = now - 3_600_000
    db = sqlite3.connect(f"file:{path}?mode=ro", uri=True, timeout=5)
    try:
        n, alerts, avg_p = db.execute("SELECT count(*), sum(alert), avg(probability) FROM predictions "
                                      "WHERE ts_ms >= ?", (hour,)).fetchone()
        last = db.execute("SELECT ts_ms, probability, model_version FROM predictions "
                          "ORDER BY ts_ms DESC LIMIT 1").fetchone()
        o = db.execute("SELECT count(*), sum(label = 1), sum(label = 0), sum(label IS NULL) FROM outcomes "
                       "WHERE ts_ms >= ?", (hour,)).fetchone()
        total = db.execute("SELECT count(*) FROM predictions").fetchone()[0]
    finally:
        db.close()
    lines = [f"last hour   : {n or 0:,} predictions, {alerts or 0} alerts, mean probability {avg_p or 0:.4f}",
             f"outcomes 1h : {o[0] or 0:,} (stress {o[1] or 0}, calm {o[2] or 0}, void {o[3] or 0})",
             f"all time    : {total:,} predictions; ledger {path.stat().st_size / 2**20:,.1f} MB"]
    if last:
        lines.append(f"latest      : p={last[1]:.4f} by {last[2]} ({(now - last[0]) / 1000:.0f}s ago)")
    return lines


def render(data_dir: Path = STATE / "data", models_dir: Path = STATE / "models", journal=journal) -> str:
    out = [f"SentinalOps live monitor | {datetime.now(UTC):%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S} UTC", "", "== SERVICES"]
    out += [f"{u:<18}: {unit_state(u)}" for u in UNITS]

    out += ["", "== LIVE PIPELINE"]
    h = last_json(journal("sentinalops-live"), "live health")
    if h:
        ev = h.get("events", {})
        out += [f"as of       : {h.get('ts')} ({age(h.get('ts'))})",
                f"events in   : depth {ev.get('depth5', 0):,}  trades {ev.get('aggTrade', 0):,}  "
                f"late {sum(h.get('late', {}).values())}",
                f"ticks       : {h.get('ticks', 0):,}  scored {h.get('predictions', 0):,}  "
                f"suspended {h.get('suspended_ticks', 0):,}  scoring errors {h.get('scoring_errors', 0)}",
                f"model       : {h.get('model_version')}  last p={h.get('last_probability')}",
                f"feed        : reconnects {h.get('reconnects', 0)}  gaps {h.get('gaps', 0)}  "
                f"drops {h.get('feed_drops', 0)}  bus drops {sum(h.get('bus_drops', {}).values())}",
                f"ledger      : written {h.get('ledger_predictions', 0):,} predictions, "
                f"{h.get('ledger_outcomes', 0):,} outcomes, errors {h.get('ledger_errors', 0)}"]
    else:
        out.append("no health line yet (first one ~60 s after start)")

    out += ["", "== HEAL"]
    hh = last_json(journal("sentinalops-heal"), "heal health")
    if hh:
        out += [f"as of       : {hh.get('ts')} ({age(hh.get('ts'))})",
                f"champion    : {hh.get('champion')}  shadow {hh.get('shadow')}",
                f"last cycle  : {hh.get('action')} ({hh.get('reason')}), matured labels {hh.get('matured')}, "
                f"live AP {hh.get('live_ap')}",
                f"history     : {hh.get('promotions', 0)} promotions, {hh.get('rollbacks', 0)} rollbacks"]
    else:
        out.append("no heal cycle yet (every 5 minutes)")

    out += ["", "== LEDGER", *ledger_section(data_dir)]

    out += ["", "== DISK AND MEMORY"]
    du = shutil.disk_usage(data_dir if data_dir.exists() else "/")
    out.append(f"disk        : {du.free / 2**30:,.1f} GB free of {du.total / 2**30:,.1f} GB")
    try:
        mem = dict(line.split(":", 1) for line in Path("/proc/meminfo").read_text().splitlines())
        total = int(mem["MemTotal"].split()[0])
        avail = int(mem["MemAvailable"].split()[0])
        used = 100 * (total - avail) / total
        flag = "" if used >= 20 else "  (under 20%: idle-reclamation risk on Always Free)"
        out.append(f"memory      : {used:.0f}% in use of {total / 2**20:.1f} GB{flag}")
    except (OSError, KeyError, ValueError):
        out.append("memory      : n/a")
    champion = models_dir / "champion.joblib"
    out.append(f"champion    : {'present' if champion.exists() else 'MISSING'} ({champion})")
    return "\n".join(out)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(render())


=== deploy\oracle\monitor.sh ===
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# deploy/oracle/monitor.sh        one snapshot
# deploy/oracle/monitor.sh live   refresh every 10 s (Ctrl-C to quit)
PY=/opt/sentinalops/venv/bin/python
HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
if [ "${1:-}" = "live" ]; then
  exec watch -n 10 -t "$PY $HERE/monitor.py"

=== deploy\oracle\preflight.py ===
"""Pre-flight: can this machine receive Binance market data? Run with the project's venv Python.

Opens the same combined stream the recorder uses for ``--seconds`` and exits 0 only if at least
one order-book snapshot and one trade arrived. Cloud IP ranges can be treated differently from
home or university networks, so ``setup.sh`` runs this before installing any service.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import asyncio
import sys
import time


def parse(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> argparse.Namespace:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    ap.add_argument("--seconds", type=float, default=10.0)
    ap.add_argument("--symbol", default="BTCUSDT")
    ap.add_argument("--base-url", default="wss://stream.binance.com:9443")
    return ap.parse_args(argv)


def verdict(counts: dict[str, int]) -> bool:
    return counts.get("depth5", 0) > 0 and counts.get("aggTrade", 0) > 0


async def listen(args: argparse.Namespace) -> dict[str, int]:
    import websockets

    from sentinalops.data.recorder.messages import build_url, parse_message

    counts = {"depth5": 0, "aggTrade": 0}
    url = build_url([args.symbol], args.base_url)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + args.seconds
    async with websockets.connect(url, open_timeout=10) as ws:
        while time.monotonic() < deadline:
            try:
                async with asyncio.timeout(max(0.1, deadline - time.monotonic())):
                    raw = await ws.recv()
            except TimeoutError:
                break
            parsed = parse_message(raw, int(time.time() * 1000))
            if parsed:
                counts[parsed[0]] += 1
    return counts


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    args = parse(argv)
    try:
        counts = asyncio.run(listen(args))
    except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 - report any network/TLS/HTTP failure plainly
        print(f"PREFLIGHT FAILED: cannot open the Binance stream: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
        print("Common causes: no outbound internet, a blocked region (HTTP 451), or a firewall.")
        return 1
    ok = verdict(counts)
    print(f"PREFLIGHT {'OK' if ok else 'FAILED'}: {counts['depth5']} order-book snapshots, "
          f"{counts['aggTrade']} trades in {args.seconds:.0f} s")
    return 0 if ok else 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

              --kafka-addr, "PLAINTEXT://0.0.0.0:9092", --advertise-kafka-addr, "PLAINTEXT://127.0.0.1:9092"]
    ports: ["127.0.0.1:9092:9092"]
    volumes: ["/var/lib/sentinalops/redpanda:/var/lib/redpanda/data"]
    restart: unless-stopped

=== deploy\oracle\setup.sh ===
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Install or update SentinalOps live on an Oracle Cloud A1 VM (Ubuntu 24.04, ARM). Idempotent.
#
#   bash setup.sh                 # in-memory bus, no Parquet archive
#   bash setup.sh --record        # also archive raw rows (a backup recorder)
#   bash setup.sh --redpanda      # Docker + Redpanda (3 GB reserved) and --bus kafka
#
# Nothing on the system changes until the Binance pre-flight check has passed.
set -euo pipefail

REPO_URL="${REPO_URL:-https://github.com/kushalradia2007/SentinalOps.git}"
BRANCH="${BRANCH:-main}"
BASE=/opt/sentinalops
REPO="$BASE/repo"
VENV="$BASE/venv"
STATE=/var/lib/sentinalops
RECORD=0
REDPANDA=0
for arg in "$@"; do
  case "$arg" in
    --record) RECORD=1 ;;
    --redpanda) REDPANDA=1 ;;
    *) echo "unknown option: $arg" >&2; exit 2 ;;
  esac
done
ME="$(id -un)"

echo "== 1/7 packages"
sudo apt-get update -qq
sudo apt-get install -y -qq git python3-venv python3-pip sqlite3 >/dev/null

echo "== 2/7 code in $REPO (branch $BRANCH)"
sudo mkdir -p "$BASE" "$STATE"/{models,data,live}
sudo chown -R "$ME":"$ME" "$BASE" "$STATE"
if [ -d "$REPO/.git" ]; then
  git -C "$REPO" fetch -q origin "$BRANCH" && git -C "$REPO" checkout -q "$BRANCH" && git -C "$REPO" pull -q --ff-only
else
  git clone -q --branch "$BRANCH" "$REPO_URL" "$REPO"
fi

echo "== 3/7 python environment"
[ -x "$VENV/bin/python" ] || python3 -m venv "$VENV"
"$VENV/bin/pip" install -q --upgrade pip
"$VENV/bin/pip" install -q -e "$REPO" aiokafka   # aiokafka for --bus kafka; nothing else needed

echo "== 4/7 pre-flight: can this VM receive Binance data?"
if ! "$VENV/bin/python" "$REPO/deploy/oracle/preflight.py" --seconds 10; then
  echo "Stopping before any system change. Fix network access to Binance, then run setup.sh again." >&2
  exit 1
fi

echo "== 5/7 champion model"
export MODEL_PATH="$STATE/models/champion.joblib"
if [ ! -f "$MODEL_PATH" ]; then
  (cd "$REPO" && "$VENV/bin/python" -m sentinalops.training.train --out "$MODEL_PATH")
else
  echo "keeping existing $MODEL_PATH"
fi

BUS=memory
if [ "$REDPANDA" = 1 ]; then
  echo "== 5b redpanda"
  if ! command -v docker >/dev/null; then
    sudo apt-get install -y -qq docker.io docker-compose-v2 >/dev/null
    sudo systemctl enable --now docker
  fi
  sudo mkdir -p "$STATE/redpanda" && sudo chown 101:101 "$STATE/redpanda"
  sudo docker compose -f "$REPO/deploy/oracle/redpanda-compose.yml" up -d
  BUS=kafka
fi

echo "== 6/7 configuration and services"
FLAGS=""
[ "$RECORD" = 1 ] && FLAGS="--record"
sudo tee /etc/sentinalops.env >/dev/null <<EOF
MODEL_PATH=$STATE/models/champion.joblib
DATA_DIR=$STATE/data
DOMAIN_CONFIG=$REPO/configs/domain_liquidity.yaml
LOG_LEVEL=INFO
KAFKA_BROKER=127.0.0.1:9092
SENTINALOPS_BUS=$BUS
SENTINALOPS_LIVE_FLAGS=$FLAGS
EOF
for unit in sentinalops-live sentinalops-heal; do
  sed "s/__USER__/$ME/" "$REPO/deploy/oracle/$unit.service" | sudo tee "/etc/systemd/system/$unit.service" >/dev/null
done
sudo systemctl daemon-reload
sudo systemctl enable sentinalops-live sentinalops-heal
sudo systemctl restart sentinalops-live sentinalops-heal
chmod +x "$REPO/deploy/oracle/monitor.sh"

echo "== 7/7 status"
sleep 5
systemctl --no-pager --lines=0 status sentinalops-live sentinalops-heal | grep -E "â—|Active:" || true
USED_PCT=$(free | awk '/^Mem:/ {printf "%d", ($3 / $2) * 100}')
echo "memory in use: ${USED_PCT}%"
if [ "$USED_PCT" -lt 20 ] && [ "$REDPANDA" = 0 ]; then
  echo "WARNING: memory use is under 20%. On an Always Free account Oracle may reclaim an idle VM."
  echo "         Upgrade the account to Pay As You Go, or run: bash setup.sh --redpanda"
elif [ "$REDPANDA" = 1 ]; then
  echo "idle-reclamation protection: Redpanda reserves 3 GB, keeping memory use above 20%"
else
  echo "idle-reclamation protection: memory use is above 20%"
fi
echo
echo "Watch it live:   $REPO/deploy/oracle/monitor.sh live"
echo "Logs:            journalctl -u sentinalops-live -f"


=== docs\DEVELOPMENT.md ===
# Development quickstart

All settings come from `configs/domain_liquidity.yaml` (typed and validated by `sentinalops.config.DomainConfig`;
unknown keys are errors). Until recorded data flows through the chunk 12 pipeline, training uses synthetic
calm and volatile regimes from `sentinalops.simulator.regimes`.

```bash
pip install -e ".[dev]"
make test                                   # pytest
ruff check .                                # lint
python -m sentinalops.training.train_baseline   # trains models/champion.joblib on the synthetic calm regime
uvicorn sentinalops.serving.app:create_app --factory --reload     # API on :8000
python scripts/smoke.py                          # scores a calm and a pre-stress tick, posts an outcome
```

Full stack: `cp .env.example .env && docker compose up -d --build`
(API :8000, MLflow :5000, Prometheus :9090, Grafana :3000).
The API image trains a baseline champion on first boot if `models/champion.joblib` is missing.

## Layout
- `configs/domain_liquidity.yaml` the domain: symbol, cadence, horizon, windows, label block, embargo, stale limit
- `src/sentinalops/config.py` typed config and env settings; `label_params()` / `feature_params()` feed the modules below
- `src/sentinalops/schemas.py` `TradeEvent`, `DepthSnapshot`, `FeatureTick`, `StressPrediction`, `OutcomeLabel`
- `src/sentinalops/data/recorder/` Binance stream recorder; `data/audit_recording.py` its audit
- `src/sentinalops/data/pipeline.py` recordings -> labelled training table (chunk 12)
- `src/sentinalops/labels.py` the stress label; `features/microstructure.py` the 34 model features
- `src/sentinalops/simulator/regimes.py` synthetic calm and volatile regimes
- `src/sentinalops/training/` baseline champion; `serving/` FastAPI app (`/score`, `/outcomes`, `/health`, `/metrics`)
- `docs/ROADMAP.md` the 50-chunk plan

## Stream recorder (chunk 7)

```bash
python -m sentinalops.data.recorder                    # record BTCUSDT trades + depth to data/raw/binance
python -m sentinalops.data.audit_recording --hours 24  # row counts, disk use, gaps, integrity
```

Details, file layout and the failure cases it handles are in [`recorder.md`](recorder.md).

## Offline feature and label table (chunk 12)

```bash
python -m sentinalops.data.pipeline        # data/raw/binance -> data/processed/features/date=*/hour=*.parquet
python scripts/bench_pipeline.py           # speed check on a synthetic day (target < 10 min)
```

Details in [`pipeline.md`](pipeline.md).


=== docs\healing.md ===
# The self-healing loop (chunks 39, 42-45)

`src/sentinalops/heal/`. Watches the champion's live quality in the prediction ledger, retrains when it degrades, promotes a challenger only if it proves itself, and rolls back if the promotion turns out worse. Settings are the `heal:` block of `configs/domain_liquidity.yaml`.

```
ledger â”€â”€â–º monitor (39) â”€â”€â–º trigger (42) â”€â”€â–º retrain (43) â”€â”€â–º gates (44) â”€â”€â–º promote + shadow (45) â”€â”€â–º keep or roll back (45)
   â–²                                                                                                          â”‚
   â””â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€ the API and stream processor hot-reload champion.joblib and score with it â—„â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”˜
```

## Monitor (chunk 39), `monitor.py`

- `window_scores(ledger, since, until, model_version)`: AP, Brier, precision, recall and base rate over matured, non-void predictions.
- `degradation(...)`: degraded when live AP over the last `metrics_window_minutes` is more than `degradation_margin` below the AP the champion scored on its holdout at promotion. Fewer than `min_positives` stress events gives no verdict (`too_few_events`), never a noisy one.

## Trigger (chunk 42), `controller.py`

`HealController.tick(now_ms)` is the whole loop, safe to run on a schedule. A retrain starts only when **all** hold:

| Rule | Why |
| --- | --- |
| Live AP degraded | Drift alone never retrains |
| At least `min_matured_labels` outcomes since promotion | Enough evidence |
| No attempt in the last `cooldown_minutes` | No thrashing |
| Not already attempted for this champion on the same evidence | Idempotent: a rejected challenger is not retrained again until `min_matured_labels` new outcomes have arrived |
| Not in a shadow period | One change at a time |

## Retrain (chunk 43), `retrain.py`

From the chunk 12 training table (`label_status == 'ok'` rows only):

- **Recent window:** the last `retrain_window_hours`.
- **Holdout:** the newest `holdout_fraction` of the window, after a `split_embargo_seconds` gap (at least the label horizon) so no training label looks into it.
- **Replay buffer:** older rows, `replay_fraction` of the recent training rows, spread evenly over regimes so the challenger does not forget them. A disjoint older sample is kept for the regression gate.

## Gates (chunk 44)

Champion and challenger are scored on the same holdout. Every gate must pass, and a gate without data fails closed.

| Gate | Default |
| --- | --- |
| Challenger AP on the current regime beats the champion's by | 0.05 |
| AP loss on older regimes at most | 0.10 |
| Challenger AP at least this multiple of the base rate (the constant baseline until chunk 26) | 1.5 |
| Brier score worse by at most | 0.01 |

The report is saved as `reports/<version>.json` and `.md` next to the registry.

## Promote, shadow, roll back (chunk 45), `registry.py`

A file-based stand-in for the MLflow registry (chunk 32): immutable `versions/`, and `champion.joblib` / `shadow.joblib` swapped atomically. Each model carries its version, so every prediction in the ledger names the model that made it.

- On promotion the old champion becomes the **shadow** for `shadow_minutes`: the stream processor scores every tick with both, and both go to the ledger under their own versions. Alerts use the champion only.
- When the shadow period ends, both are compared on the same ticks. If the new champion's AP is worse by `rollback_margin`, the old one is restored automatically; otherwise the shadow is retired.
- `registry.count("promote")` and `count("rollback")` back the `model_promotions_total` / `model_rollbacks_total` counters; every event is in `registry.json`.

## Tests (`tests/test_heal.py`)

The main test runs the whole story on a simulated market (`simulator/heal_sim.py`): 90 minutes calm, then 150 minutes volatile, scored tick by tick through the ledger.

| Minutes | What the loop did |
| --- | --- |
| 0-90 calm | Idle; live AP about 0.5-0.7 |
| 95-100 | Shift: live AP falls to about 0.1-0.2; degradation detected |
| 100 | First challenger **rejected**: only about 10 minutes of volatile data, so it failed the gain and baseline gates |
| 105-120 | Cool-down, then "already attempted" |
| 125 | Second challenger **promoted** (+0.49 AP, all gates pass); old champion goes to shadow |
| 145 | Shadow review: new model better, kept |
| 150-240 | Healthy again; live AP about 0.6-0.69 |

A second test force-promotes a bad model and checks it is rolled back after the shadow period. Mutation check: promoting without the gates, never rolling back, or dropping the embargo each fail a test.

## Not done yet

- Prefect flow and schedule around `tick()` (42.1, 42.6), MLflow logging and registry (43.5, 32), Prometheus counters (45.4), drift scores as a warning input (42.2 with chunk 38).
- Calibration of the challenger (31) and the chunk 26 persistence baseline in the gates.


=== docs\labels.md ===
# The liquidity-stress label (chunk 9)

This is the definition of what the model predicts. The offline pipeline (chunk 12) and the outcome verifier (chunk 34) both call `src/sentinalops/labels.py`, so training labels and live verification cannot drift apart.

**Status:** the code tasks (9.1, 9.2, 9.4, 9.5) are done and tested on synthetic data. The thresholds below are still placeholders. Tasks 9.3, 9.6 and 9.7 need at least a few days of recorded depth data and are not done.

## Definition

Everything happens on a one-second grid.

1. **Book state.** From each `depth5` snapshot: mid-price, relative spread, and top-5 depth (the sum of quantities across five bid and five ask levels). Crossed or empty books are dropped.
2. **Tick.** Tick `t` takes the newest snapshot received at or before `t`. If that snapshot is more than 3 s old, the tick is **unknown**.
3. **Reference.** The rolling median of depth and of spread over the previous 600 s, excluding the current tick. It needs 300 known ticks before it is trusted.
4. **Stressed.** `depth < depth_fraction Ã— reference depth` (primary), or `spread > spread_multiple Ã— reference spread` (secondary).
5. **Confirmed.** Stressed for 2 ticks in a row. A single-snapshot flicker is not an event.
6. **Label at `t`.** For a tick that is calm at `t`: 1 if confirmed stress occurs anywhere in `(t, t + 30 s]`, otherwise 0.

| Case | Label |
| --- | --- |
| Calm now, confirmed stress within 30 s | 1 |
| Calm now, no confirmed stress within 30 s | 0 |
| Already stressed at `t` | not eligible |
| Any tick in the horizon is unknown (feed gap, warm-up, end of data) | void |

The module also produces `time_to_onset_s` (for the lead-time metric) and two regression targets, `fwd_max_rel_spread` and `fwd_min_depth_ratio`.

## Why it is built this way

- **Onset, not state.** Stress persists. If stressed ticks were eligible, "stressed now" would predict "stressed in 30 s" almost perfectly and every model would look good. Excluding them makes the task a forecast.
- **Void, never guessed.** A label whose horizon crosses a feed gap is dropped. Treating missing data as "no stress" would teach the model that outages are calm.
- **Reference excludes the present.** A collapse at `t` must not lower the bar it is measured against.
- **Long-lived stress fades from the label.** The reference is a 10-minute median, so if depth stays low for more than about five minutes the reference adapts and the market counts as calm again. This is intended: the label means "abnormal relative to recent conditions".

## What the tests prove

`tests/test_labels.py` checks the rules above, including:

- Truncating the data at any point leaves every causal column at earlier ticks unchanged.
- A label is identical whether or not data beyond its own horizon exists.
- The fast vectorised labels equal a slow, obvious per-tick implementation (`label_at`) on every tick of a noisy series with gaps. The verifier in chunk 34 has to match `label_at`.

Four deliberate bugs were injected to confirm the tests catch them: a reference that sees one tick ahead, a horizon that includes the current tick, stressed ticks made eligible, and ticks sampling the next snapshot. Each one failed between 3 and 7 tests.

## Running it on recorded data

```bash
python -m sentinalops.labels --data-dir data/raw/binance --symbol BTCUSDT
python -m sentinalops.labels --data-dir data/raw/binance --fit --stress-share 0.01
```

The first command uses the thresholds in `configs/domain_liquidity.yaml`. It prints the base-rate report followed by go/no-go notes. The report covers:

- how often each rule fires;
- eligible ticks and positives;
- stress episodes per hour and their median length;
- the share of positives that came from the single busiest hour;
- how well a lazy baseline ("the thinner the book is now, the likelier stress is") already ranks the positives.

`--fit` picks thresholds so each rule fires on about that share of ticks. If the spread never leaves one tick it switches the spread rule off.

## Still to decide with real data

| Task | Question | Needs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 9.3 | Final `depth_fraction` and `spread_multiple`, fitted on the training period only, then frozen in the config with the date range used | Several days of recording |
| 9.6 | Is the base rate workable? Aim for a few hundred episodes, a positive rate between 0.2% and 20%, and positives not concentrated in one burst | Same |
| 9.6 | Does the spread rule contribute anything on BTCUSDT, or is depth the whole label? | Same |
| 9.7 | If the label is too rare: longer horizon, softer threshold, or a second symbol | The 9.6 report |

Fit thresholds only on the training period. Fitting on all data and then testing on part of it is leakage.


=== docs\live.md ===
# Live system

`src/sentinalops/live/`. Connects to Binance, scores a forecast every second, verifies each forecast 30 s later, and keeps the model healthy. Design: `docs/design/model-slot-and-live-wiring-design.md`.

```
Binance (aggTrade + depth5@100ms)
   â”‚  BinanceFeed: the recorder's reconnect, watchdog, 23 h rotation, de-duplication
   â–¼
 raw_trades, raw_depth â”€â”€â–º features worker (StreamProcessor) â”€â”€â–º features, predictions
 raw_depth â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â–º outcomes worker (OutcomeVerifier)  â”€â”€â–º outcomes
 predictions, outcomes â”€â–º ledger writer â”€â”€â–º data/ledger.sqlite (append-only)
 clock: every 1 s, so ticks keep coming (and are suspended) when the feed goes silent

 heal service (separate process): every 5 min, HealController.tick(now)
   reads the ledger, may retrain the champion's own recipe, promotes through the gates,
   writes models/champion.joblib, and the live process hot-reloads it
```

## Processes

| Command | What runs |
|---|---|
| `python -m sentinalops.live` | Feed, features, outcomes and ledger in one process (in-memory bus) |
| `python -m sentinalops.live --role heal` | The heal service. Caps its BLAS/OpenMP threads at 1 |
| `python -m sentinalops.live --bus kafka --role feed` (or `features`, `outcomes`, `ledger`) | One component per process over Redpanda |

Common options are `--record` (also archive raw rows to Parquet), `--data-dir` (feed state and gap log), and `--health-interval-s` / `--heal-interval-min`. Paths come from the usual environment variables: `MODEL_PATH`, `DATA_DIR`, `DOMAIN_CONFIG`, and `KAFKA_BROKER` for Kafka.

## Behaviour worth knowing

- **Warm-up.** Predictions start about 60 s after connecting (the longest feature window). Outcomes are `warmup` voids for the first 5 minutes, until the label's trailing reference is trusted.
- **Feed silent or disconnected.** After `stale_data_seconds` (3 s) the ticks are suspended (no predictions). The verifier voids any horizon that crosses the gap. The feed reconnects with backoff, and recovery needs a fresh 60 s warm-up.
- **No model file.** Features keep flowing and scoring errors are counted. Predictions start as soon as `champion.joblib` appears.
- **Restarts.** Prediction ids are derived from symbol, tick and model version, so re-scored ticks never duplicate ledger rows.
- **Manual model upgrade.** Train with `python -m sentinalops.training.train` (see `docs/models.md`). The heal service registers the new `champion.joblib` as a version and promotes it ("manual install"). From then on, heal cycles retrain that model's own recipe.
- **Backpressure.** Every queue is bounded and drops its oldest item if a consumer stalls (counted as `bus_drops` / `feed_drops` in the health line). This should never happen at about 20 messages a second.

## Health

`live health` (every 60 s) logs events in, late events (must stay 0), ticks, scored and suspended, the model version and last probability, reconnects, gaps, drops, ledger writes and errors, and the last heal event. `heal health` (every cycle) logs the champion and shadow, the last action and its reason, matured labels, live AP, and the promotion and rollback counts.

## Oracle Cloud (Always Free, Ampere A1)

1. Create an Ubuntu 24.04 **ARM** VM (VM.Standard.A1.Flex, 2 OCPU / 12 GB, boot volume 150 GB or more) in an **India** home region. Binance blocks US locations, and the home region cannot be changed later.
2. SSH in and run:

   ```bash
   git clone https://github.com/kushalradia2007/SentinalOps.git && cd SentinalOps
   BRANCH=main bash deploy/oracle/setup.sh            # add --redpanda and/or --record
   ```

   The script checks Binance access first (`deploy/oracle/preflight.py`) and changes nothing on the system if that fails. It then installs two systemd units, `sentinalops-live` and `sentinalops-heal`, which restart on failure and start at boot.
3. Watch it: `/opt/sentinalops/repo/deploy/oracle/monitor.sh live`.

**Idle reclamation.** Oracle may reclaim an Always Free VM whose CPU, network and memory use all stay under 20% for 7 days. This system's CPU and network use stay far below that. Either upgrade the account to Pay As You Go (Always Free resources stay free), or run with `--redpanda`, which reserves 3 GB of memory. The setup script and the monitor both show which applies.


=== docs\models.md ===
# Models: the plug-in slot

The system around the model is fixed. A model is a package in `src/sentinalops/models/`, and the rest of the system only knows the contract below.

## The contract (`models/base.py`)

A package exposes a **recipe**: `recipe.fit(train_df, cfg, seed=0, params=None)`. It returns a **fitted model** with:

| Requirement | Why |
|---|---|
| `predict_proba(X: DataFrame) -> (n, 2)`, column 1 = stress probability | The API, stream processor and gates call it |
| `classes_ == [0, 1]` and `feature_names_in_ == cfg.feature_names` (the 34 raw features) | The API refuses a model with other inputs |
| A writable `sentinal_version_` | The registry stamps the version, so every prediction names its model |
| `sentinal_model_name_` and `sentinal_model_params_` (use `base.stamp`) | The heal loop retrains the champion with **its own** recipe |

The recipe owns everything about training: preprocessing, derived features (computed from the 34 inputs only), class weights, tuning, calibration. `base.check_fitted(model, cfg)` checks the contract.

## Frozen packages

Pickles refer to classes by import path. Never change a package once models trained with it are in a registry, because old versions and rollbacks must keep loading. An upgrade is a **new package** (`gbm_v2`), never an edit of `gbm_v1`.

## Upgrade a model in 3 steps

1. Add `src/sentinalops/models/<new_name>/__init__.py` exposing `recipe`, and add one line to `_RECIPES` in `src/sentinalops/models/__init__.py`.
2. Set `model.name: <new_name>` (and `params`) in `configs/domain_liquidity.yaml`.
3. Train and install it:

   ```bash
   python -m sentinalops.training.train --data data/processed/features   # or --data synthetic
   ```

   This writes `models/champion.joblib` atomically. The API and stream processor hot-reload it. Under the live system, stop the heal service, replace the champion, and start the heal service: it registers the new file as the champion.

## How the heal loop chooses the recipe

`heal/retrain.py::resolve_recipe` uses the champion's `sentinal_model_name_` and `sentinal_model_params_`. Editing the config never changes what a heal cycle trains. Only champions trained before the model slot existed (no stamp) fall back to `model.name` from the config.

## Models

| Name | What | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `gbm_v1` | scikit-learn `HistGradientBoostingClassifier(max_iter=200, learning_rate=0.08)` | The original champion; pickles depend on scikit-learn only |


=== docs\outcomes.md ===
# Prediction ledger and outcome verifier (chunk 34)

The loop that lets the platform grade itself: every prediction is stored, the verifier works out what actually happened once the 30 s horizon has passed, and the ledger joins the two.

## Outcome verifier

`src/sentinalops/stream/verifier.py`. Consumes the raw depth stream (labels depend only on the book) and, for every tick `t`, emits an `OutcomeLabel` to `outcomes` once tick `t + 30 s` has been seen.

It is the chunk 9 label computed incrementally: as-of book state per tick, the trailing 600 s median reference (previous ticks only, at least 300 known), `stressed`, `confirmed` (two stressed ticks in a row), then the onset label from ticks `t+1 .. t+30`.

| `label` | `void_reason` | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | - | stress started within 30 s |
| 0 | - | it did not |
| null | `already_stressed` | the market was stressed at `t`; onset is undefined |
| null | `gap` | the book went stale in the horizon (or just before `t`) |
| null | `warmup` | the trailing reference was not established yet |

Watermark, allowed lateness and late-event counting work like the stream processor (`docs/streaming.md`).

**Tests:**

- Replaying 30 minutes of depth (two stress episodes and an 8 s outage) gives the same label as offline `build_labels` on every one of 1,769 ticks, and matches the slow reference `label_at` on a 200-tick sample. Mutation check: letting the reference include the current tick, or dropping the confirmation rule, fails the test.
- An outcome is never emitted before the watermark passes `t + 30 s`; deleting data after time T never changes an outcome whose horizon ended before T.

The offline training table also voids labels whose horizon overlaps an outage in the recorder's gap log; the live verifier relies on staleness only.

## Prediction ledger

`src/sentinalops/serving/ledger.py`, an SQLite file (`data_dir/ledger.sqlite` for the API).

- **Append-only:** `predictions` and `outcomes` are insert-only; SQLite triggers reject any update or delete. A prediction gets its outcome by joining on `(symbol, ts_ms, horizon_s)`, so nothing is written back into a prediction row.
- **Idempotent:** a replayed prediction (same ID) or a second outcome for the same tick is ignored, so it can consume an at-least-once topic.
- **Feature snapshot:** stored with each prediction for explanations and drift checks.

Each prediction ends as `hit`, `miss`, `false_alarm`, `correct_negative` or `void`, and is `pending` until its outcome arrives. Void rows are excluded from every metric.

`Ledger.scores(since_ms, until_ms, model_version)` returns the confusion table, precision, recall, base rate and Brier score for a time window; chunk 39 builds the rolling monitoring metrics on it.

## Still to do

- `GET /predictions/{id}` and `GET /predictions?since=` (task 34.6, Tanuj): `Ledger.get` and `Ledger.predictions` already return what they need.
- Wiring the verifier and the ledger to Redpanda topics (chunk 15).


=== docs\pipeline.md ===
# Offline feature and label pipeline (chunk 12)

`python -m sentinalops.data.pipeline` turns the recorder's Parquet files into the training table: one row per second with the 34 features, the stress label and the regression targets.

## Output

```
data/processed/features/date=2026-11-15/hour=13.parquet   one file per recorded UTC hour
data/processed/features/_runs/run-<time>.json              summary of each run
```

Read it all with `sentinalops.data.pipeline.read_table(out_dir)`, or `pd.read_parquet` on the directory.

| Column | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `symbol`, `ts_ms` | the tick (UTC epoch ms, whole seconds) |
| 34 features | `FEATURE_ORDER` from `features/microstructure.py`; never null |
| `label` | 1 if stress starts in `(t, t+30 s]`, 0 if not, null when `label_status` is not `ok` |
| `label_status` | `ok`, `not_calm` (already stressed at t), `void_unknown` (stale feed or warm-up in the horizon), `void_gap` (a recorded outage in the horizon) |
| `time_to_onset_s`, `fwd_max_rel_spread`, `fwd_min_depth_ratio` | regression targets from chunk 9 |
| `regime` | `untagged` until chunk 14 |
| `source` | `recorded_binance_stream` |

The schema is `table_schema()`; `validate_table()` checks it and runs before every write.

## Rules

- **Rows:** a tick is kept only if its features are valid (fresh book, 60 s of continuous feed) and no outage in the recorder's gap log overlaps `(t - 60 s, t]`. An outage can lose trades while the book still looks fresh, so the gap log is checked as well as staleness.
- **Labels:** a label is kept only if no logged outage overlaps `(t, t + 30 s]`. Gaps scoped to another symbol are ignored.
- **Hours are independent:** each hour is built from the previous, the same and the next hour's files with enough padding for the 600 s label reference, the 60 s windows and the 30 s horizon. A test checks that the hour files equal one pass over the whole recording.
- **Resumable:** finished hours are skipped (`--force` rebuilds). Files are written to `.tmp` then renamed.
- **The newest hour is left out** because its last labels need the next hour; pass `--include-last` once the recording has stopped.

## Leakage tests

- Deleting all data after time T leaves every row whose horizon ends before T unchanged.
- Sampled rows match the slow reference `compute_tick`.
- Mutation check: removing the feature-window gap check, the horizon gap check, or the padding each makes a test fail.

## Speed (task 12.6)

`scripts/bench_pipeline.py` on a synthetic day at real rates (864,000 snapshots, about 1.7 million trades): **6.7 s per day** on a Windows laptop, against a 10-minute target.


=== docs\recorder.md ===
# Stream recorder (chunk 7)

Records Binance Spot `aggTrade` and `depth5@100ms` for each symbol to Parquet. Spot order-book depth has no public archive, so this runs continuously from day one. It uses public market data only: no API key, no account endpoints.

## Run it

```bash
python -m sentinalops.data.recorder                      # BTCUSDT into data/raw/binance
python -m sentinalops.data.recorder --symbols BTCUSDT,ETHUSDT --metrics-port 9108
python -m sentinalops.data.recorder --duration 60        # smoke test: stop after 60 s
docker compose up -d recorder                            # long-running, restarts on failure
python -m sentinalops.data.audit_recording --hours 24    # the task 7.9 report
```

Every flag has a `RECORDER_*` environment variable (see `--help`). Stop with Ctrl-C, `SIGTERM`, or Ctrl-Break on Windows; buffered rows are flushed before exit.

## What is on disk

```text
data/raw/binance/
  stream=aggTrade/symbol=BTCUSDT/date=2026-11-15/hour=13/part-<first_ms>-<last_ms>-<id>.parquet
  stream=depth5/symbol=BTCUSDT/date=2026-11-15/hour=13/part-<first_ms>-<last_ms>-<id>.parquet
  _gaps/gap-<start_ms>-<id>.parquet     one row per outage: start_ms, end_ms, duration_ms, reason, scope
  _state/last_seen.json                 last receive time, used to log restart gaps
  _rejected/frames.jsonl                malformed frames, kept for inspection (capped at 5 MB)
```

Partitions use the local receive time in UTC. One file is written per partition per flush (every 60 s by default, or earlier at 50,000 buffered rows), with Zstandard compression.

| Table | Columns |
| --- | --- |
| `aggTrade` | `symbol`, `event_time_ms`, `trade_time_ms`, `agg_trade_id`, `price`, `quantity`, `first_trade_id`, `last_trade_id`, `buyer_is_maker`, `recv_time_ms` |
| `depth5` | `symbol`, `last_update_id`, `recv_time_ms`, then `bid_px_1`, `bid_qty_1` ... `bid_px_5`, `bid_qty_5` and the same for `ask` |

Times are UTC epoch milliseconds (`int64`); prices and quantities are `float64`. The depth stream carries no exchange timestamp, so `recv_time_ms` is its only clock. `buyer_is_maker = true` means the trade was sell-initiated.

## How it avoids losing data

| Risk | What the recorder does |
| --- | --- |
| Connection drops | Reconnects with exponential backoff and jitter (0.5 s doubling to 30 s) and writes the outage to `_gaps/` |
| Connection open but silent | A watchdog reconnects after 10 s with no frames, or 10 s with no depth frame for any symbol |
| Binance's 24-hour connection limit | At 23 hours it opens a replacement connection first, then closes the old one; overlapping rows are de-duplicated by trade ID and update ID |
| Crash during a write | Files are written to `*.tmp`, fsynced and renamed; leftovers are removed at startup |
| Write fails (for example, disk full) | The rows go back into the buffer and are retried on the next flush |
| Shutdown | Buffers are flushed before exit |
| Process was down | On restart, the time since the last recorded frame is written as a `restart` gap |
| Malformed frame | Counted, logged and kept in `_rejected/`; never dropped silently |
| Wrong endpoint | Only `stream.binance.com` and `data-stream.binance.vision` are allowed |

A hard kill or power loss still loses whatever was buffered since the last flush (up to 60 s). That loss shows up as a `restart` gap.

## Health

A `recorder health` JSON log line is written every 30 s with messages per second per stream, buffered rows, last flush latency, reconnects, rotations, gaps, duplicates, malformed frames and the last event timestamp. With `--metrics-port`, the same values are served as Prometheus gauges named `recorder_*`.

## The audit report

`audit_recording` prints three sections and exits 1 on any problem:

1. **Volume:** files, rows, megabytes and rows per second for each symbol and stream, with a projected size per day.
2. **Gaps:** for depth, any receive-time jump above 500 ms; for trades, any hole in the aggregate trade IDs. Each gap is matched against `_gaps/`. A gap the recorder did not log is reported as unexplained.
3. **Integrity:** schema match, null counts, duplicate or out-of-order rows, unreadable files, leftover `.tmp` files.

Trade time gaps are not reported, because a quiet market produces them without any loss. A hole in the trade IDs is the reliable sign.


=== docs\ROADMAP.md ===
# SentinalOps Roadmap: 50 chunks

Every open chunk is split into small tasks in [`TASKS.md`](TASKS.md). Owners are in [`TEAM_PLAN.md`](TEAM_PLAN.md).

**Domain (locked): Liquidity Intelligence.** SentinalOps watches the BTCUSDT order book and trades on Binance's public market-data streams. It detects abnormal liquidity conditions, forecasts liquidity stress 30 seconds ahead, verifies every forecast against the future stream, and heals the model when the market regime changes.

This is an early-warning and monitoring system. It is not a trading bot: it places no orders, needs no API keys and makes no price predictions.

## What changed from the supply-chain plan

- Chunks 1, 4, 5 and 6 stay done. The platform code they built is domain-neutral.
- Chunks 2, 3, 7, 8 and 9 were finished for the old domain and are **reopened** with new content. The old code (`features/extract.py`, `simulator/zero_day.py`, `data/malwarebench.py`, `data/qut_dv25.py`, `data/fetcher.py`) is removed in task 2.6 once its replacements exist.
- Chunk numbers and owners are unchanged, so each person keeps the same lane.

## Design rules that every chunk follows

1. **No future data in features.** A prediction at time `t` uses only events with timestamp at or before `t`. The verifier labels it using only events after `t`.
2. **Depth depletion is the primary label.** BTCUSDT usually trades at a one-tick spread, so spread expansion is a secondary label. Chunk 9 measures both base rates before anything is trained.
3. **Label the onset of stress.** A market that is already stressed is not a forecast. Every model is also compared with a persistence baseline ("stressed now, so stressed in 30 seconds").
4. **Predict once per second.** Horizons overlap, so train and test splits are chronological with an embargo gap of at least the horizon.
5. **Drift alone does not retrain.** Retraining needs a measured drop in matured-label performance; feature drift is supporting evidence.
6. **Record first.** There is no public archive of spot order-book depth, so the recorder (chunk 7) runs from day one.
